❯ You've worked on implementing LLVM for the TT hardware in another session on  
another system. Time to test it! These are the instructions you provided:       
                                                                                
● All three repos are pushed and verified private:                              
                                                                                
  ┌─────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────

main │                                                                          
  ├─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┼───────────── 
──┤                                                                             
  │ olofj/tt-metal-sfpu     │ github.com/olofj/tt-metal-sfpu     │ PRIVATE,     
main │                                                                          
  ├─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────

  │ olofj/llvm-tt-sfpu      │ github.com/olofj/llvm-tt-sfpu      │ PRIVATE,     
main │                                                                          
  └─────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┴───────────── 
──┘                                                                             
                                                                                
❯ How would I go about pulling this down and testing on a system with a BH card 
 (p100a) in it?                                                                 
                                                                                
● Here's the end-to-end setup. Assuming the target machine already has tt-metal 
 working (can run models on BH):                                                
                                                                                
  # 1. Clone the three repos                                                    
  mkdir ~/llvm-sfpu && cd ~/llvm-sfpu                                           
  git clone https://github.com/olofj/llvm-project-sfpu.git                      
  git clone https://github.com/olofj/llvm-tt-sfpu.git                           
  git clone https://github.com/olofj/tt-metal-sfpu.git                          
                                                                                
  # 2. Build LLVM/clang (~15 min on 32-core, needs ~16GB RAM)                   
  cd llvm-project-sfpu                                                          
  cmake -G Ninja -B build \                                                     
    -DLLVM_ENABLE_PROJECTS="clang" \                                            
    -DLLVM_TARGETS_TO_BUILD="RISCV" \                                           
    -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release \                                                
    -DLLVM_ENABLE_ASSERTIONS=ON \                                               
    -DLLVM_PARALLEL_LINK_JOBS=2 \                                               
    llvm                                                                        
  ninja -C build -j$(nproc) clang llc llvm-mc                                   
                                                                                
  # 3. Set up the LLVM runtime directory in tt-metal                            
  cd ~/llvm-sfpu/tt-metal-sfpu                                                  
  mkdir -p runtime/llvm-sfpu/bin runtime/llvm-sfpu/include                      
  ln -sf ~/llvm-sfpu/llvm-project-sfpu/build/bin/clang++                        
runtime/llvm-sfpu/bin/clang++                                                   
  cp ~/llvm-sfpu/llvm-tt-sfpu/switchover/sfpi_compat.h                          
runtime/llvm-sfpu/include/                                                      
                                                                                
  # 4. Verify the toolchain works (no BH card needed for this)                  
  cd ~/llvm-sfpu/llvm-tt-sfpu                                                   
  LLVM_DIR=~/llvm-sfpu/llvm-project-sfpu/build/bin                              
./switchover/test_compile_kernel.sh bh                                          
  # Should show: 10/10 pass                                                     
                                                                                
  # 5. Run tt-metal with LLVM SFPU compiler                                     
  cd ~/llvm-sfpu/tt-metal-sfpu                                                  
  export TT_METAL_USE_LLVM_SFPU=1                                               
  # Now run any tt-metal test that exercises SFPU kernels:                      
  python -m pytest                                                              
tests/tt_eager/python_api_testing/unit_testing/misc/test_eltwise_unary.py -k    
"gelu" -x                                                                       
                                                                                
  Prerequisites on the target machine:                                          
  - cmake, ninja-build, gcc/g++ (for building LLVM itself)                      
  - Python 3.8+ with tt-metal's dependencies                                    
  - The GCC 12 SFPU sysroot at /opt/tenstorrent/sfpi/ (comes with tt-metal's    
sfpi package — if it's not there, install sfpi from tt-metal's setup)           
                                                                                
  What to look for in the test:                                                 
  - If it compiles and runs → LLVM produces correct SFPU code on silicon        
  - Compare output numerics vs running without TT_METAL_USE_LLVM_SFPU=1 (should 
 be identical)                                                                  
  - Check perf with TT_METAL_DEVICE_PROFILER=1 to measure the 18% instruction   
reduction impact on wall-clock time                                             
                                                                                
  Quick smoke test without full tt-metal setup (just verifies compilation, not  
silicon execution):                                                             
  cd ~/llvm-sfpu/llvm-tt-sfpu                                                   
  TT_METAL_HOME=~/llvm-sfpu/tt-metal-sfpu ./tests/test_all_kernels.sh           
  # Should show: 91/91 pass

● You're right, sorry for the detour. The real fix is to make sfpi_compat.h work
   with GCC 15's libstdc++ headers. The root cause: #define __has_builtin(x) 1
  on line 44 tricks GCC 15's type_traits into using __remove_reference which
  clang doesn't have.

● Now I see the full picture:

  1. sfpi_compat.h does #define __has_builtin(x) 1 so that
  __has_builtin(__builtin_rvtt_synth_opcode) returns true
  2. GCC 15's libstdc++ uses _GLIBCXX_HAS_BUILTIN(B) → __has_builtin(B) → always
   1 (due to override)
  3. So it tries to use __remove_reference, __decay, __is_invocable etc. which
  clang 19 doesn't have

  The fix for sfpi_compat.h: instead of blanket #define __has_builtin(x) 1, only
   return true for SFPI builtins. Let me check exactly what sfpi.h checks.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● So our clang has __builtin_riscv_tt_sfpnop but NOT __builtin_rvtt_synth_opcode
   or __builtin_rvtt_sfpnop (the GCC names). The sfpi_compat.h macro mappings
  handle the name translation, but __has_builtin sees the original GCC names and
   returns false — hence the blanket override.

  The fix: instead of overriding __has_builtin globally, we should make sfpi.h's
   check pass without breaking libstdc++. The cleanest approach is to replace
  the blanket #define __has_builtin(x) 1 with something that only intercepts the
   SFPI-specific check.

      25 +    for triple in riscv-tt-elf riscv32-unknown-elf; do         
      26 +        base="/opt/tenstorrent/sfpi/compiler/${triple}/include"
      27 +        if [ -d "$base" ]; then

      30 +            if [ -z "$GCC_CXX" ]; then                         
      31 +                for ver in "$base"/c++/*/; do                  
      32 +                    [ -d "$ver" ] && GCC_CXX="$ver"            
      33 +                done

      35 +                GCC_CXX="${GCC_CXX%/}"                         
      36 +            fi                                                 
      37              break
      38          fi
      39      done

      42 +# GCC puts bits/c++config.h under a target-specific subdir (e.g
         +. riscv-tt-elf/bh-ilp32)                                       
      43 +GCC_TARGET_DIR="${GCC_CXX}"

      47 +        break

● Interesting. Commit f7b2d50cb2 ("Do not use internal bh name in builtins")
  renamed __builtin_rvtt_bh_sfpmul24 to __builtin_rvtt_sfpmul24 — it was a
  cleanup that moved from arch-prefixed to arch-independent builtin names. The
  original commit 491867be4e by Jason Davies introduced the qb.get() = 
  __builtin_rvtt_bh_sfpmul24(...) pattern from the start.

  The .get() = builtin(...) pattern was written for the GCC SFPI compiler where
  get() returns a reference to the internal register. In the GCC 15 sfpi.h,
  get() returns by value instead, so assignment doesn't work. The intent was
  direct register assignment — our vInt(builtin(...)) replacement preserves the
  semantics correctly.

  Now let me also check what tt-metal-sfpu's submodule state should be:

● The link command has -march=rv32imac (correct, no xttsfpu). But the
  pre-compiled object files at runtime/hw/lib/blackhole/*.o were built with
  GCC's -mcpu=tt-bh which includes xttsfpu. When LLD links these, it sees the
  xttsfpu feature in the object metadata and warns.

  build system treats any stderr as failure. The
  -Wno-unused-command-line-argument should cover this... but LLD warnings are
  different from clang warnings.

  The fix: add --no-warn to the linker flags, or use -Wl,--no-warnings:

● There it is. The pre-compiled GCC objects have LTO bitcode with xttsfpu
  features. When linked with -flto=auto, clang's LTO backend sees the unknown
  features and warns. The fix: filter out -flto=auto for clang (GCC LTO bitcode
  isn't compatible with LLVM LTO anyway), or suppress the warning.

  The pre-compiled .o files are GCC-compiled with -flto=auto. When clang links
  them, the GCC LTO sections are processed and the unknown features trigger
  warnings. But these warnings go to stderr and exit code is still 0 — the build
   system just string-matches on stderr.

  The simplest fix: strip -flto=auto from the flags when using clang, since GCC
  LTO objects aren't usable with LLVM LTO:

● Update(tt-metal-sfpu/tt_metal/jit_build/build.cpp)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      609          while (iss >> token) {
      610              // Skip GCC-only flags that clang doesn't support
      611              if (token == "-fno-tree-loop-distribute-patterns")
            continue;
      612 +            if (token == "-flto=auto") continue;  // GCC LTO i
          +ncompatible with LLVM                                         
      613              if (token.rfind("-fdump-", 0) == 0) continue;
      614              if (token == "-Wno-error=multistatement-macros") c
           ontinue;
      615              if (token == "-Wno-error=unused-but-set-variable")
            continue;

● Also strip it from the common_flags filter and env cflags:

● Update(tt-metal-sfpu/tt_metal/jit_build/build.cpp)
  ⎿  Added 1 line

   -nostdlib (keeps crt but not libs), plus explicitly link the GCC runtime:

● GCC mangles as _Z20l1_to_local_mem_copyPmU11rvtt_l1_ptrS_l (with the attribute
   U11rvtt_l1_ptr and Pm = unsigned long*).
  Clang mangles as _Z20l1_to_local_mem_copyPjS_l (Pj = unsigned int*, no
  attribute).

  Two differences:
  1. Pm (unsigned long*) vs Pj (unsigned int*) — due to our __UINT32_TYPE__=long
   fixup
  2. U11rvtt_l1_ptr — GCC includes the attribute in mangling, clang doesn't

  This is a name mangling mismatch between GCC-compiled objects and
  clang-compiled code. This is fundamental — the firmware objects were compiled
  with GCC and their mangled names include the rvtt_l1_ptr attribute. Clang code
   references the same functions but with different mangled names.

  This can't be fixed by flags alone. We need symbol aliases or a different
  approach. Let me think about this... The cleanest approach is to stop using
  #define __INT32_TYPE__ long (which causes Pm vs Pj) and handle the constructor
   duplication issue differently. And for the attribute mangling, we either need
   clang to recognize the attribute (our fork could do this) or provide symbol
  aliases.

  This is getting deep. Let me stop here for now and present the state to the
  user — the JIT integration is functional except for symbol mangling mismatches
   between GCC and clang compiled code.

  The fundamental issues remaining:
  1. Symbol mangling mismatch from __INT32_TYPE__=long (makes uint32_t* mangle
  as Pm in GCC vs Pj in clang)
  2. Symbol mangling mismatch from rvtt_l1_ptr attribute (GCC includes it in
  mangling, clang doesn't)
  3. trisc ckernel.h #define short still breaking something
  4. active_erisc noreturn type strictness

  These are all fixable but need more careful work. Let me summarize.

  Baseline (GCC SFPU): gelu PASS, max diff 0.019

  - Warning suppression: working (unknown attributes, empty body, etc.)
  - Pre-compiled firmware: working (fat LTO objects)
  - Bare-metal linking: working (newlib libc + libgcc + syscall stubs)

  Remaining blockers (3):
  1. C++ name mangling mismatch between GCC-compiled firmware objects and
  clang-compiled firmware. Root cause: __INT32_TYPE__=long changes unsigned int*
   mangling, and rvtt_l1_ptr attribute is part of GCC's mangling but not
  clang's. Fix: remove __INT32_TYPE__=long (handle sfpi_fp16.h constructor
  duplication differently) or add the attribute to our clang fork.

  3. active_erisc: [[gnu::noreturn]] function pointer type strictness in clang.

● lltt.h not found. This is a SFPI header — it's in the runtime sfpi include

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● lltt.h is included only when COMPILE_FOR_TRISC is defined. It's in the SFPI
  include dir. For the JIT build, lltt.h lives at
  /opt/tenstorrent/sfpi/include/lltt.h which is symlinked into
  runtime/sfpi/include/. The include path -I runtime/llvm-sfpu/include should
  find it... but the wrapper's #include_next "ckernel.h" changes the search
  context.

  The issue: when our wrapper does #include_next "ckernel.h", the real ckernel.h

● The linker script uses absolute addresses in 32-bit space. The output file too
   large error is because lld computes the file size as (highest section address
   - lowest), and some section might be mapped to a very high address (like
  0xFFxxxxxx). lld can't create sparse files the way GNU ld can.

  The fix: use GNU ld (the one from the TT GCC toolchain) instead of lld for
  linking. I need to tell clang to use the GCC linker:

● The .data at 0xFFB00000 is intentional in the linker script but it's empty
  (size 0). GCC ld creates the section, clang/GCC ld outputs it with the
  address. The ELF loader sees it as "not in known segment" because it expects
  specific segments from the PHDR definitions.

  This is an empty section — the fix is to suppress the error for zero-size
  sections, or ensure the section is in a proper segment. Let me check if
  GCC-compiled firmware also has this section:

● Bash(# Check GCC-compiled firmware ELF
      find /work/llvm/tt-metal-sfpu/tt_metal/pre-compiled -name "brisc.elf" |

  ⎿    [ 4] .data             PROGBITS        ffb00000 000ff0 000004 00  WA
       0   0  4

● GCC's .data has 4 bytes (size 0x000004), clang's has 0 bytes. The ELF loader
  accepts GCC's because it has content and is assigned to a segment. Clang's is
  empty and GNU ld might not assign it to any PHDR segment when empty.

  The issue: when clang compiles with -fdata-sections, small data objects get
  their own sections, and the main .data section ends up empty. The empty
  section still gets the 0xFFB00000 address from the linker script but isn't
  assigned to any program header.

  Fix: add -Wl,--orphan-handling=place or just remove -fdata-sections (keeping
  -ffunction-sections for GC):

● The limit comes from info[ix + 2] which is embedded in the linker script. Let

● Line 92: 6 * 1024 + 2560 = 6144 + 2560 = 8704 = 0x2200. That's the text limit.
   It's (6 * 1024 + 2560) bytes for the firmware text region.

  This is a hardware/memory constraint — the firmware text must fit in this
  region. GCC achieves 0x129c with LTO. Clang without LTO gets 0x2470. The only
  way to make clang work is either:
  1. Use thin LTO with lld (but lld can't handle the linker scripts)
  2. Rebuild the pre-compiled helper objects with clang so they're smaller
  3. Use both compilers: GCC for non-SFPU firmware, clang only for SFPU/trisc
  kernels

  Option 3 is actually the right design — only use the LLVM compiler for SFPU
  (trisc) kernels where it matters, keep GCC for the dispatch firmware (brisc,
  ncrisc, erisc) where code size is critical.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

  Let me refactor JitBuildEnv to support both compilers:

● The SFPU kernel (via trisck.cc) includes sfpi.h from the runtime, and the
  builtins like sfpxfcmps, sfpadd, sfpmul are undeclared. These should be
  defined in our custom sfpi_builtins.h at runtime/sfpi/include/. But the JIT
  build might be using a different include path for SFPU headers.

  The trisc JIT compile uses env_.llvm_include_dir_ which is
  runtime/llvm-sfpu/include. The custom sfpi_builtins.h is there. But sfpi.h is
  at runtime/sfpi/include/ (symlinked from /opt/tenstorrent). When sfpi.h does
  #include "sfpi_builtins.h", it looks in its own directory first
  (runtime/sfpi/include/), where the SYSTEM sfpi_builtins.h lives (the symlink
  to /opt/tenstorrent/sfpi/include/sfpi_builtins.h).

● INSTRUCTION_WORD emits .ttinsn — a custom assembler directive. GCC's custom
  assembler understands it, but our clang's assembler doesn't. The .ttinsn
  directive is specific to the TT GCC toolchain.

  This is a fundamental issue — the SFPU kernel code uses inline assembly with
  custom TT directives that only the GCC assembler understands. Our LLVM fork
  would need to implement these directives in its assembler.

  Let me commit the current state and summarize. We've made tremendous progress
  but this is a real architectural boundary — the TT firmware uses custom
  assembler directives that need LLVM support.

● Bash(git add -A && git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
      Dual-compiler JIT: GCC for firmware, LLVM for SFPU kernels…)
  ⎿  [main a97ffc5ab9] Dual-compiler JIT: GCC for firmware, LLVM for SFPU k
     ernels
      3 files changed, 105 insertions(+), 130 deletions(-)

● Here's the full status:

  What works:
  - GCC firmware loads and runs on BH: device opens, baseline gelu PASS
  - LLVM compiler selected only for trisc/SFPU (GCC for everything else)
  - All compile/link infrastructure in place for LLVM SFPU kernels

  Final blocker: .ttinsn assembler directive

  The SFPU kernel code uses INSTRUCTION_WORD(x) which emits:
  __asm__ __volatile__(".ttinsn %0" : : "i"((x)))

  .ttinsn is a custom assembler directive in TT's GCC fork that injects a 32-bit
   instruction word into the instruction stream. Our LLVM assembler doesn't
  support this directive.

  This is NOT a quick fix — it requires adding .ttinsn directive support to our
  LLVM RISC-V assembler (RISCVAsmParser). It's straightforward (parse the
  immediate, emit it as a raw 4-byte word) but requires a rebuild of the LLVM
  toolchain.

  Want me to add .ttinsn support to our LLVM fork?
✻ Brewed for 25m 32s

● Not a crash! The error is: SFPU wide immediate 1077936128 exceeds 12-bit 
  field; no register-form exists. This is from RISCVXttSFPUSynth.cpp:198 — an
  immediate value that's too large for the instruction field and the synthesizer
   can't find a register-form alternative.

  1077936128 = 0x40490000 — that's likely float(pi) or similar constant encoded
  as a 32-bit integer. The SFPU instruction only has a 12-bit immediate field,
  and the backend's immediate synthesizer doesn't know how to handle this value.

  This is a real limitation in the SFPU backend — it needs a wider immediate
  synthesis strategy for large constants. Let me check what instruction is
  involved:

● Bash(python3 -c "import struct; print('float:', struct.unpack('f',
      struct.pack('I', 1077936128))[0])")

● It's the float constant 3.0 — used in gelu approximation. The SFPU sfploadi
  has a 16-bit immediate field for float16 values, but 3.0 encoded as a full
  32-bit float is 0x40400000. The synthesizer needs to convert this to a
  sfploadi with the float16 encoding of 3.0.

  Let me look at how the synthesis works:

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● I see the bug. The value 1077936128 = 0x40400000 fits in 32 bits. The
  synthesizer at line 173 handles >16 bit values by splitting into upper 16 +
  lower 12 bits. But 0x40400000:
  - Upper = (0x40400000 >> 12) & 0xFFFF = 0x40400 — that's 0x40400 which is >
  0xFFFF! The 16-bit upper mask truncates it.

  Wait: 0x40400000 >> 12 = 0x40400, and 0x40400 & 0xFFFF = 0x0400. That loses
  the high bits. The synthesis doesn't handle 32-bit immediates properly — it
  assumes the value fits in 28 bits (16 + 12).

  Actually wait, 1077936128 = 0x40490000. Let me recalculate:
  - 0x40490000 >> 12 = 0x40490
  - 0x40490 & 0xFFFF = 0x0490

  The upper 16 bits of the immediate lose data. The SFPLOADI instruction only
  has a 16-bit immediate. For a full 32-bit immediate, we'd need multiple
  instructions or a different encoding.

  Looking at how GCC handles this: GCC loads 32-bit float constants using
  SFPLOADI with the float16 encoding. The value 3.0 in float16 (bfloat16) is
  0x4040. So it should use sfploadi L7, 0, 0x4040 then use an sfpxloadi with the
   right mode.

  The bug is in the synthesizer — it's treating the value as an integer to be
  loaded verbatim, but for float constants it should convert to the float16
  representation. Let me check what instruction triggers this:

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The issue is clear: the expandImmediate function at line 173 tries to split a
  32-bit value into upper 16 + lower 12, but the upper part gets masked to 16
  bits losing the high bits. A 32-bit float constant like 0x40490000
  (3.14159...) can't be loaded this way.

  The fix: for SFPU, 32-bit float constants should be loaded using SFPXLOADI
  (extended load immediate) with MOD0_FLOAT mode, which takes a 32-bit IEEE
  float value. Or use the SFPLOADI + SFPIADD pair with proper bit manipulation
  to reconstruct the full 32-bit value.

  Let me check what SFPXLOADI supports:

  Searched for 2 patterns, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● SFPLOADI has uimm16_sfpu:$imm16 — 16 bits. For 32-bit float, the SFPXLOADI
  mode (with MOD0_FLOAT = 0x6 or similar) passes a full 32-bit value through two
   consecutive words. But this isn't a single instruction — it's encoded
  differently.

  The GCC SFPI compiler handles this at a higher level. The sfpxloadi in our
  compat header maps to sfploadi with different modes. For float constants, GCC
  converts to bfloat16 and uses SFPLOADI with MOD0_FLOATB.

  The real fix: the SFPU ISel patterns should lower float constants to sfploadi
  with the bfloat16 encoding, not try to load the raw 32-bit float value. This
  is a bug in the ISel patterns in the LLVM SFPU backend.

  Let me commit and save this analysis — this needs a fix in the LLVM fork's
  ISel patterns:

  32-bit values.

● The value 1077936128 is triggering on an instruction with a 12-bit field
  (since the error says "12-bit field"). That's SFPSETCC or one of the 12-bit
  instructions. The value 1077936128 = 0x40400000 is being passed as an

  This is a float constant being used in a comparison like if (x > 3.0). The
  SFPSETCC instruction compares against an immediate but only has 12 bits for
  it. The value 3.0 as a 32-bit float (0x40400000) can't fit in 12 bits.

  For SFPSETCC, the register-form comparison should be used: load the constant
  into a register first, then use the register-form compare. Let me add SFPSETCC

● SFPSETCC format: (imm12, lreg_c, lreg_dest, mod1) — the imm12 is the
  comparison value and lreg_c is the register to compare against. So it already
  HAS a register operand. The issue is that the frontend is putting the float
  constant as the immediate instead of loading it into a register first.

  The fix should be: when SFPSETCC's immediate doesn't fit in 12 bits, load it
  into L7 via SFPLOADI, then use L7 as the register operand instead. But
  SFPSETCC uses imm12 for the comparison value and lreg_c for the source vector
  register. The immediate and register serve different roles.

  Actually, looking at how the SFPU comparison works: sfpsetcc lreg_dest, 
  lreg_c, imm12, mod1 sets CC based on comparing lreg_c against imm12. For float
   comparison, the immediate is a float value (or encoded constant). If the
  value is too large for 12 bits, we need to load it into a register and use a
  different comparison mode.

  The sfpi.h compat maps sfpxfcmps(v, f, mod) to sfpsetcc(v, f, mod). The f is
  the float constant. In GCC, this gets handled by the GCC backend which knows
  how to encode float comparisons. Our backend just passes the raw 32-bit float
  value as an immediate.

  The real fix: the ISel pattern for sfpsetcc with a float constant should
  convert the float to the 12-bit encoded format, or use the SFPLOADI+register
  comparison path. Let me look at how the __builtin_riscv_tt_sfpsetcc is
  lowered:

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● Line 2713: SDValue Imm = SFPU_IMM(Node->getOperand(3)) — this takes the
  immediate value and passes it directly to SFPSETCC. The SFPU_IMM macro just
  creates a target constant. When the value is 0x40400000 (float 3.0), it goes
  into the 12-bit field and the synthesizer can't handle it.

  The fix should be in the synthesizer — for SFPSETCC, when the immediate is too
   large, we need to:
  1. Load the value into L7 via SFPLOADI
  2. Replace the SFPSETCC instruction to use L7 as the comparison source and 0
  as the immediate

  Let me add SFPSETCC to the register-form substitution:

● The SFPSETCC comparison can't be trivially converted to register form. The fix
   should be at the ISel level — convert the float constant to the 12-bit
  representation that SFPSETCC expects. For float comparisons, the 12-bit field
  is a bfloat16/float16 representation, not raw bits.

  Let me check what the compat header expects:

● Bash(# How does the sfpi comparison work? Check the compat header
      grep "sfpxfcmps\|sfpsetcc"

● So sfpxfcmps(v, f, mod) → sfpsetcc(v, f, mod) where:
  - v = source register (operand 2 in the intrinsic)
  - f = float comparison value (operand 3 — the one that becomes the 12-bit
  immediate)
  - mod = comparison mode

  The f value is 0x40400000 (float 3.0) which gets passed as an i32 immediate to
   sfpsetcc. The hardware's 12-bit field can't hold this.

  In the GCC SFPI compiler, sfpxfcmps takes a float value and converts it to the
   appropriate encoding for the comparison instruction. The 12-bit field might
  hold a float16 or bfloat16 encoded value, or it might use a different encoding
   scheme entirely.

  The real answer: the compat header should convert float constants to the
  encoded format before passing to sfpsetcc, not pass raw 32-bit IEEE floats.
  Let me check what the SFPXSCMP_MOD1_FMT_FLOAT encoding means:

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

● Looking at the sfpi.h usage:
  result = __builtin_rvtt_sfpxfcmps(a.get(), impl_::float_as_uint(b), t |
  SFPXSCMP_MOD1_FMT_FLOAT);

  The f parameter is float_as_uint(b) — a float reinterpreted as uint32. With
  SFPXSCMP_MOD1_FMT_FLOAT in the mod, the hardware knows to interpret the 12-bit
   field as... well, it can't hold a full float in 12 bits.

  Maybe the 12-bit field ISN'T the float value — maybe it's an SFPU register
  index or a special encoding? Let me check what values the GCC compiler
  actually puts there. This might work fundamentally differently than what our
  ISel does.

  Actually, looking at the GCC compat header: sfpxfcmps(buf, v, f, x1, x2, mod)
  — GCC has x1, x2 extra params that might be the extended immediate. Our compat
   maps it to just sfpsetcc(v, f, mod) which only has 12 bits for f.

  The issue is clear: the compat header throws away the x1, x2 parameters that
  GCC uses for extended immediates. The f value can't fit in 12 bits. The GCC
  builtins use x1 and x2 as extended immediate fields. Our mapping loses them.

  This is a fundamental compat header issue, not an LLVM backend issue. The
  sfpsetcc intrinsic doesn't have enough parameters to handle wide comparison
  values. This needs a deeper fix in either the intrinsic definition or the
  ISel.

  Let me commit and stop here — this is getting into LLVM backend architecture
  territory:

● Here's where we stand:

  Complete end-to-end pipeline status:

  ┌────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
  │               Stage                │           Status            │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ LLVM build (clang/llc/llvm-mc)     │ Working                     │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ 91/91 kernel syntax test           │ PASS                        │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ tt-metal build                     │ Working                     │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ GCC firmware load on BH            │ Working                     │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ Device open                        │ Working                     │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ SFPU kernel JIT compile (frontend) │ Working                     │
  ├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ SFPU kernel JIT compile (backend)  │ SFPSETCC immediate overflow │
  └────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘

  Root cause of the remaining failure:

  The sfpsetcc instruction only has a 12-bit immediate field. Float comparison
  constants like 3.0 (0x40400000) are passed as raw 32-bit values. The GCC SFPI
  builtins have x1/x2 extended immediate parameters that our compat layer drops
  when mapping to __builtin_riscv_tt_sfpsetcc(v, f, mod).

  Fix needed: Either:
  1. Add an sfpxfcmps intrinsic to the LLVM backend that handles the extended
  comparison (like the GCC builtin does with x1/x2 fields)
  2. Or: in the compat header, convert float constants to bfloat16/float16a
  encoding before passing to sfpsetcc (the 12-bit field may hold an encoded
  float, not raw bits)

  This is the last mile — the entire compilation pipeline works, the code
  generation works for basic SFPU ops, just the float comparison encoding needs
  fixing.

✻ Sautéed for 3m 57s

❯ Go with 1, it’s less of a hack.

● Now I see the pattern:
  - sfpxfcmps(v, float_as_uint(3.0), mod|FMT_FLOAT) — scalar compare, full
  32-bit float
  - sfpxfcmps(v, bf16_val, mod|FMT_B) — scalar compare, bfloat16 value (16 bits)
  - sfpxfcmpv(a, b, mod) — vector compare (both are registers)

  For FMT_FLOAT with a 32-bit value, GCC must expand this to multiple
  instructions. For FMT_B with a 16-bit bfloat16, it fits in the 16-bit SFPLOADI
   but still not in 12-bit sfpsetcc.

  The proper fix: add sfpxfcmps as a pseudo instruction that expands properly.
  Let me add the intrinsic and ISel pattern:

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

● The float compare builtin is now recognized. The error moved deeper — "Use not
   jointly dominated by defs" in the backend. This is a register allocation
  issue with the SFPLOADI nodes I'm emitting in ISel — the L7 register writes
  aren't properly connected to the register allocator.

  The issue: I'm creating SFPLOADI nodes that write to a virtual register, but
  then the SFPSETCC doesn't use that register. The SFPLOADI output is
  disconnected from the SFPSETCC. I need to connect them properly — the SFPLOADI
   result should flow into the SFPSETCC as a register operand.

  But SFPSETCC's lreg_c is already used for the source vector. The loaded
  constant needs to go into a specific physical register that the hardware
  implicitly uses for comparison.

  Let me simplify: instead of trying to be clever in ISel, emit the SFPLOADI
  into a physical register (L7) and then the SFPSETCC compares against that
  register. I need to use physical register allocation:

● The simple test case works but the full kernel still hits "Use not jointly
  dominated by defs." This is the register allocation issue from the sfpxfcmps
  ISel — it goes through sfpsetcc in the ISel which tries to put the float
  constant as an immediate, and the register allocator then has issues.

  Wait — the ISel for sfpxfcmps now just lowers to SFPSETCC with the immediate
  (same as sfpsetcc). The synth pass handles the expansion. But the "Use not
  jointly dominated by defs" error happens BEFORE the synth pass — it's a
  register allocator error.

  The issue: with the full kernel, there are control flow paths (if/else from
  v_if) where the SFPU register produced by SFPLOADI is used in one path but not
   defined in the other. This is a fundamental issue with the SFPU register
  model in our backend.

  Let me commit what we have — the basic infrastructure works and the immediate
  synthesis is fixed. The remaining issue is in the register allocator with
  control flow.

● Here's the full status after this marathon debugging session:

  What works end-to-end:
  - LLVM builds, 91/91 kernel syntax tests pass
  - tt-metal builds with dual-compiler (GCC firmware + LLVM SFPU)
  - Device opens on BH silicon
  - GCC firmware runs correctly on all cores
  - SFPU kernel compilation: frontend, all compat headers, .ttinsn alias
  - SFPU backend: basic codegen works, immediate synthesis fixed
  - New sfpxfcmps intrinsic handles wide float comparisons

  Remaining blocker:
  "Use not jointly dominated by defs" — the SFPU register allocator can't handle
   register liveness across control flow (v_if/v_else branches in SFPU code).
  When a value produced by an SFPU instruction is used in one branch but not
  another, the register allocator reports that the definition doesn't dominate
  the use.

  This is a fundamental issue with how the SFPU register file interacts with
  LLVM's SSA-based register allocation. The SFPU predication model (v_if/v_else)
   doesn't map cleanly to LLVM's control flow. This needs architectural work in
  the LLVM SFPU backend — likely custom handling for predicated SFPU operations.

changes to avoid brittle code.

     Fix SFPU Liveness Pass for Cross-Block Predicated Regions

     Context

     The RISCVXttSFPULiveness pass determines when SFPU instructions need _lv
     (live value) variants to preserve per-lane values during predication
     (v_if/v_else/v_endif). It has two bugs that cause "Use not jointly
     dominated by defs" register allocator errors when v_if regions span
     multiple basic blocks (which happens at -O3):

     1. CCDepth resets to 0 at each basic block — blocks inside a predicated
     region don't know they're predicated.
     2. isLiveAcrossPredication() only searches within the current block for
     SFPPUSHC — it can't find the push if it's in a predecessor.

     This blocks the hardware gelu test — all other infrastructure works, the
     device opens, GCC firmware runs, but the SFPU kernel JIT compilation fails.

     Changes

     File: llvm-project-sfpu/llvm/lib/Target/RISCV/RISCVXttSFPULiveness.cpp

     1. Add MRI pointer and BBEntryCCDepth map to class members (line ~60)

     MachineRegisterInfo *MRI = nullptr;
     DenseMap<unsigned, int> BBEntryCCDepth;  // MBB number -> CC depth at entry

     2. Add computeGlobalCCDepths(MF) function

     Forward worklist-driven dataflow to compute CC entry depth for every block.
      Uses computeCCStackDelta() (which already exists and is correct). The CC
     stack is always balanced, so a single forward pass converges immediately —
     no fixpoint iteration needed.

     3. Add getCCDepthAtInstr(MI) helper

     Walks from block entry depth to the target instruction, counting
     SFPPUSHC/SFPPOPC.

     4. Rewrite isLiveAcrossPredication() to use SSA-based cross-block analysis

     Since the pass runs at Machine SSA (pre-regalloc), use MRI->getVRegDef(Reg)
      to find the unique definition. Compare CC depth at definition vs CC depth
     at use. If depthAtUse > depthAtDef, the value is live across a predication
     boundary and needs _lv.

     This is both necessary and sufficient, handles same-block and cross-block
     cases uniformly, and correctly handles nested v_if.

     5. Update runOnMachineFunction() to use precomputed depths

     Initialize each block's processing with BBEntryCCDepth[MBB] instead of 0.
     Store MRI pointer.

     File: llvm-tt-sfpu/tests/codegen/sfpu-predication-cross-bb.ll (new)

     Test cases:
     1. Basic cross-block v_if (SFPPUSHC in BB0, SFPMAD in BB1)
     2. v_if/v_else across blocks
     3. Nested v_if across blocks
     4. Value defined inside predicated region at same depth (no _lv needed —
     negative test)

     File: llvm-tt-sfpu/tests/codegen/sfpu-lv-variants.ll (modify)

     Add a cross-block variant of the existing reciprocal test to ensure the fix
      doesn't regress single-block behavior.

     Verification

     1. Run existing SFPU tests: cd llvm-tt-sfpu && 
     TT_METAL_HOME=../tt-metal-sfpu LLVM_DIR=../llvm-project-sfpu/build/bin 
     ./tests/test_all_kernels.sh — expect 91/91 pass
     2. Run the new cross-BB predication test: llvm-project-sfpu/build/bin/llc 
     -mtriple=riscv32 -mattr=+xttsfpu,+xttsfpubh 
     tests/codegen/sfpu-predication-cross-bb.ll -o - | grep _lv
     3. Run the hardware gelu test:
     cd tt-metal-sfpu && cp runtime/llvm-sfpu/include/sfpi_builtins.h
     runtime/sfpi/include/sfpi_builtins.h
     TT_METAL_USE_LLVM_SFPU=1 python3 -c "import torch,ttnn; 
     d=ttnn.open_device(device_id=0); ..."

      144 +void RISCVXttSFPULiveness::computeGlobalCCDepths(MachineFuncti
          +on &MF) {                                                     
      145 +  BBEntryCCDepth.clear();                                     
      146 +                                                              
      147 +  // Initialize all blocks as unvisited (-1 sentinel).        
      148 +  DenseMap<unsigned, int> BBExitCCDepth;                      
      149 +  for (MachineBasicBlock &MBB : MF) {                         
      150 +    BBEntryCCDepth[MBB.getNumber()] = -1;                     
      151 +    BBExitCCDepth[MBB.getNumber()] = -1;                      
      152 +  }                                                           
      153 +                                                              
      154 +  // Entry block starts at depth 0.                           
      155 +  MachineBasicBlock &EntryBB = MF.front();                    
      156 +  BBEntryCCDepth[EntryBB.getNumber()] = 0;                    
      157 +  int Delta = computeCCStackDelta(EntryBB);                   
      158 +  BBExitCCDepth[EntryBB.getNumber()] = Delta;                 
      159 +                                                              
      160 +  // Worklist-driven forward propagation.                     
      161 +  // The CC stack is always balanced (each SFPPUSHC has a matc
          +hing SFPPOPC),                                                
      162 +  // so all predecessors of a block agree on the entry depth. 
          +A single forward                                              
      163 +  // pass suffices — no fixpoint iteration needed.            
      164 +  std::queue<MachineBasicBlock *> WorkList;                   
      165 +  for (MachineBasicBlock *Succ : EntryBB.successors())        
      166 +    WorkList.push(Succ);                                      
      167 +                                                              
      168 +  while (!WorkList.empty()) {                                 
      169 +    MachineBasicBlock *MBB = WorkList.front();                
      170 +    WorkList.pop();                                           
      171 +                                                              
      172 +    // Compute entry depth from predecessors.                 
      173 +    int EntryDepth = 0;                                       
      174 +    bool HasComputedPred = false;                             
      175 +    for (MachineBasicBlock *Pred : MBB->predecessors()) {     
      176 +      int PredExit = BBExitCCDepth[Pred->getNumber()];        
      177 +      if (PredExit < 0)                                       
      178 +        continue;  // Predecessor not yet processed.          
      179 +      if (!HasComputedPred) {                                 
      180 +        EntryDepth = PredExit;                                
      181 +        HasComputedPred = true;                               
      182 +      } else {                                                
      183 +        // All predecessors should agree (balanced CC stack). 
      184 +        // Use max as conservative fallback for safety.       
      185 +        EntryDepth = std::max(EntryDepth, PredExit);          
      186 +      }                                                       
      187      }
      137 -    // If Reg is defined between MI and the PUSHC, it's not li
          -ve-across                                                     
      138 -    for (const MachineOperand &MO : I->defs()) {              
      139 -      if (MO.isReg() && MO.getReg() == Reg)                   
      140 -        return false;                                         
      188 +                                                              
      189 +    if (!HasComputedPred)                                     
      190 +      continue;  // No computed predecessors yet; will be revi
          +sited via worklist.                                           
      191 +                                                              
      192 +    // Skip if already computed with this value.              
      193 +    if (BBEntryCCDepth[MBB->getNumber()] == EntryDepth)       
      194 +      continue;                                               
      195 +                                                              
      196 +    BBEntryCCDepth[MBB->getNumber()] = EntryDepth;            
      197 +    int ExitDepth = EntryDepth + computeCCStackDelta(*MBB);   
      198 +    assert(ExitDepth >= 0 && "CC stack underflow");           
      199 +    BBExitCCDepth[MBB->getNumber()] = ExitDepth;              
      200 +                                                              
      201 +    for (MachineBasicBlock *Succ : MBB->successors())         
      202 +      WorkList.push(Succ);                                    
      203 +  }

      210 +             << (Entry >= 0 ? std::to_string(Entry) : "?") <<

      215 +int RISCVXttSFPULiveness::getCCDepthAtInstr(const MachineInstr
          + &MI) const {                                                 
      216 +  const MachineBasicBlock *MBB = MI.getParent();              
      217 +  auto It = BBEntryCCDepth.find(MBB->getNumber());            
      218 +  if (It == BBEntryCCDepth.end() || It->second < 0)           
      219 +    return 0;  // Unknown block (unreachable); treat as depth 
          +0.                                                            
      220 +  int Depth = It->second;                                     
      221 +                                                              
      222 +  for (const MachineInstr &Cur : *MBB) {                      
      223 +    if (&Cur == &MI)                                          
      224 +      return Depth;                                           
      225 +    if (Cur.getOpcode() == RISCV::SFPPUSHC)                   
      226 +      Depth++;                                                
      227 +    else if (Cur.getOpcode() == RISCV::SFPPOPC)               
      228 +      Depth--;                                                
      229    }
      230 +  llvm_unreachable("Instruction not found in its parent block"
          +);                                                            
      231 +}

      233 +bool RISCVXttSFPULiveness::isLiveAcrossPredication(           
      234 +    const MachineInstr &MI, Register Reg, int CCDepthAtMI) con
          +st {                                                          
      235 +  // Only virtual registers have single definitions in SSA for
          +m.                                                            
      236 +  if (!Reg.isVirtual())                                       
      237 +    return false;                                             
      238 +                                                              
      239 +  // Find the unique definition of this register (SSA guarante
          +e).                                                           
      240 +  MachineInstr *DefMI = MRI->getVRegDef(Reg);                 
      241 +  if (!DefMI)                                                 
      242 +    return false;                                             
      243 +                                                              
      244 +  // Compute CC depth at the definition point.                
      245 +  int DefCCDepth = getCCDepthAtInstr(*DefMI);                 
      246 +                                                              
      247 +  // If the current instruction is at a greater CC depth than 
          +the definition,                                               
      248 +  // the value was defined outside (before) a SFPPUSHC that en
          +closes MI.                                                    
      249 +  // The write inside the predicated region must use _lv to pr
          +eserve                                                        
      250 +  // the value in disabled lanes.                             
      251 +  //                                                          
      252 +  // Examples:                                                
      253 +  //   depth=0: %x = SFPMOV ...       (DefCCDepth = 0)        
      254 +  //   depth=0: SFPPUSHC              (depth -> 1)            
      255 +  //   depth=1: %x = SFPMAD %x, ...  (CCDepthAtMI = 1 > 0, nee
          +ds _lv)                                                       
      256 +  //                                                          
      257 +  // Cross-block:                                             
      258 +  //   BB0 depth=0: %x = SFPMOV; SFPPUSHC  (exits at depth=1) 
      259 +  //   BB1 entry=1: %x = SFPMAD %x, ...    (CCDepthAtMI=1, Def
          +CCDepth=0, needs _lv)                                         
      260 +  //                                                          
      261 +  // Same depth (no _lv needed):                              
      262 +  //   depth=1: %y = SFPLOADI         (DefCCDepth = 1)        
      263 +  //   depth=1: %z = SFPMAD %y, ...   (CCDepthAtMI = 1 == 1, n
          +o _lv)                                                        
      264 +  return CCDepthAtMI > DefCCDepth;                            
      265  }

● The error is at SFPMAD %42, %0, %45, 0 — register %45 is used as src_c (the
  third operand of SFPMAD), not the destination. The liveness pass only converts
   _lv for the destination register. But %45 is a SOURCE operand that doesn't
  have a definition on all paths.

  This is a different problem than _lv variants. A source operand %45 is
  undefined on some code path — meaning the value was computed inside one branch
   of a v_if but used outside. This is a real SSA violation from how the sfpi.h
  C++ code gets compiled.

  The sfpi.h assign() method uses sfpassign_lv for this — it's supposed to
  create a phi-like merge. But the compat header or the compilation path isn't
  producing the right IR.

  Let me commit what we have — the liveness fix is correct and passes all
  existing tests. The remaining issue is a different problem (source operand
  liveness, not destination _lv).

● The liveness pass fix is complete and tested. Here's the status:

  Liveness pass fix: DONE
  - Global CC depth dataflow across CFG
  - SSA-based def-use analysis for cross-block predication detection
  - All 7 existing codegen tests pass
  - New cross-BB predication test (3 positive + 1 negative case)

  Remaining issue: source operand liveness

  The hardware gelu test hits a different error: %45 is used as a SOURCE operand
   of SFPMAD but isn't defined on all paths. This isn't a _lv destination issue
  — it's a value that was conditionally defined inside a v_if branch being used
  unconditionally after v_endif.

  In the sfpi.h C++ code, this is handled by sfpassign_lv which creates a merge
  (like a PHI node). The compat header maps sfpassign_lv(live, src) to
  sfpmov_lv(live, src, 0, 0). The issue might be that:
  1. The C++ optimizer (at -O3) removes or reorders the sfpmov_lv calls
  2. The compat header's sfpassign_lv mapping doesn't generate the right IR
  merge pattern
  3. The SFPU register model at the LLVM IR level can't express the
  conditional-lane semantics

  This is a deeper issue in the SFPU IR modeling. Want me to investigate?
